Build two hand-curated join tables once: (a) sector name → stadium seating chart → is-this-the-ultras-end, from public club ticketing maps; (b) sanction → the specific future fixture it lands on ("next UEFA home match" must be resolved against the draw). Total volume is a few hundred rows a season — a scraper plus a weekend of manual mapping. All of it is published public record; nothing here touches any individual's private behavior.
- **The bet / trade:** Single-match, settles at full time. Primary leg on the affected fixture: back HOME TEAM TOTAL CARDS / booking points OVER, paired with AWAY TEAM CARDS UNDER (or take the away side of the team-cards handicap / "most cards" market) whenever the home ultras sector is closed. Secondary leg, sized only on full closures and behind-closed-doors orders: AWAY TEAM Asian handicap +0.25 / draw-no-bet away, plus UNDER 0.5 home-team penalties. Reverse leg on the Osservatorio away-ban set: back AWAY TEAM CARDS OVER, since the visiting support that partially offsets home crowd pressure is gone. Bets placed as soon as the document publishes if the match is already up, otherwise at market open.
- **Mechanism:** Crowd noise, specifically sustained coordinated noise from the ultras end, biases referees toward the home team. The COVID ghost-game natural experiment measured this cleanly across 40,000+ matches: with fans absent, foul calls against the home team rose ~26% while calls against the away team rose only ~3%; yellow-card differentials moved against the home side in most countries; the home-win share fell from 43.8% to 41.2%; and studies of a full season under attendance restrictions confirmed spectators drive both home advantage and referee bias. Remove the specific sector that generates the noise and you get a scaled version of the same shift: home fouls and cards up, away cards down, home penalty rate down, home advantage down. The key non-obvious point is that the relationship is to noise, not to headcount — so the sanction that closes one curva delivers far more of the ghost-game effect than its ~6% capacity loss would suggest, and any model keyed on attendance figures will underweight it badly.
- **Who's on the other side:** The odds compilers and their feed vendors. Card and booking-points markets are priced off referee card priors, team foul rates, and derby/importance flags — attendance is not an input to the automated pricing at all, and even full behind-closed-doors games are handled by a trader manually reacting to English-language news. Industry guides openly state card markets get "far less attention from the bookmaker's trading team than goals markets," which is exactly where a small unmodeled effect survives. The documents themselves are Turkish and Italian legal PDFs on federation and Ministry of the Interior websites that no betting operation scrapes. Sharp syndicates that did build crowd-effect models in 2020-21 mothballed them when crowds returned; the partial-closure residual case is too small and too manual to justify rebuilding. And the suspended-sanction structure hides half the signal: clubs sit on deferred closures for 1-2 year probations (Real Madrid, Benfica, Bayern, Derry City all carrying one in 2025-26), so a closure can activate off a fresh incident weeks before any journalist connects it back to the old probation.
- **Information lead:** Osservatorio determinazioni: ~10-14 days before the fixture; local Italian press picks it up 1-3 days later, English-language coverage usually never. FIGC Giudice Sportivo: Tuesday for the following weekend, sometimes multiple giornate ahead, so 4-12 days. TFF PFDK: typically 1-2 weeks before the affected home match. UEFA CEDB is the outlier and the real prize: a sanction is worded as applying to "the next UEFA competition match the club plays as host," so a decision published in March against a club that then gets eliminated lands on its first home European match of the FOLLOWING season — a fixture that has not even been drawn yet. That is a 3-6 month lead on a match the book will not price for months and, when it does, will price without ever reading a March PDF. Same structure on the block bans: Juventus and Torino supporters were given 10-matchday away bans in the summer covering fixtures through 3 November. For the domestic cases the honest exploitable window before the card line would move on its own is 5-20 days; for the UEFA carry-forwards the book effectively never learns it at all and simply misprices the match on the day.
- **Why still under-exploited:** Nobody sells it. Sportradar and Genius sell referee feeds, lineups, and injury data; there is no commercial "which sector is closed" product, and searches for anyone trading stadium closures or away-fan bans turn up only news coverage and generic courtsiding articles. The academic crowd-effect work is public but was framed as a one-off pandemic natural experiment, not an ongoing tradable signal, so it was read and filed rather than operationalized. The practical barriers are exactly the kind that repel scale players: three-plus languages, unstructured and sometimes scanned PDFs, government and federation sites with no API, a sanction-to-fixture mapping that requires human reasoning about competition draws and probation status, and a sector-to-acoustics table you must hand-build from seating charts and ultras-group locations. The dataset is a few hundred rows a season — beneath the notice of anyone building industrially, and perfectly sized for one determined person. Finally, these sanctions surface publicly as moral gossip ("club fined for racist chanting") rather than as what they actually are: a structured, dated, binding operational fact about the acoustic environment of a specific future match.
- **Rough +EV:** Effect scales with how much of the noise is removed. Partial single-curva closure: expect roughly +0.10-0.15 home yellows and -0.05-0.10 away yellows, which is ~3-4 percentage points of probability on a home-cards-over-1.5 line — against typical 5-8% two-way card-market margins that is about +1.5-3% ROI on the best-priced book, and near zero at the worst. Full closure or behind-closed-doors: the full ghost-game effect, ~8-12pp on home card overs and ~2.5pp of home-win probability, worth roughly +4-7% on cards and +1-2% on the Asian handicap. Away-fan-ban reverse leg is the weakest, maybe +1-2%. Frequency: roughly 80-150 qualifying fixtures per season pooling Serie A/B, Süper Lig, Greek Super League, UEFA club competitions and the Osservatorio away-ban set, with the EV heavily concentrated in the 10-20 largest closures. Blended expectation ~+2.5-4% ROI. Practical constraint: card-market limits are low (hundreds to low thousands per bet) and books restrict consistent winners in these niches quickly, so this is a good-percentage, modest-absolute-dollars edge unless spread across many accounts and exchanges.
- **Confidence:** Medium. The data sources are verified real and scrapeable, the lead time is unambiguous, and the direction of the effect is about as well-established as anything in sports economics. The single biggest uncertainty is dose-response: every published effect size comes from FULL closures, and the partial-closure extrapolation assumes the noise-to-bias relationship is roughly linear in the ultras sector's contribution. It may instead be sharply nonlinear — 45,000 remaining fans might generate enough pressure that silencing one curva does almost nothing, which would collapse the partial-closure leg (the bulk of the sample) and leave only the rare full closures as tradable. This is directly testable before risking money: pool every partial closure and away-fan ban across Italy, Turkey, Greece and UEFA from ~2015 onward against match card and result data, and check the card differential. Secondary risks: the sanction-to-fixture mapping is error-prone on UEFA carry-forwards, clubs sometimes get closures overturned on appeal after publication, and card-market limits plus fast account restriction cap the strategy's absolute capacity.

---
## 35. The Murrum Requisition

**Arena:** gambling — cricket betting (Betfair exchange + Asian/Indian books; IPL, PSL, BPL, internationals, and BCCI domestic)
**Domain:** Cricket & subcontinent betting markets. RULES: institutions/venues only; sanctioned collection

- **Data source:** Cricket ground PROCUREMENT records — the tender notices, corrigenda and work orders that state cricket associations and government stadium owners are legally required to publish when they buy pitch soil, relay a square, stitch in a hybrid surface, re-turf an outfield, or install sub-surface drainage. These documents name the exact soil class being bought ("Black Soil, Red Soil (Sieved) & Murrum (Sieved)", "fine textured black cotton clay suitable for construction of cricket pitches"), the venue, and the season it is for — i.e. they disclose a structural change in how a venue will play, months before a ball is bowled on it. Verified live examples: Punjab CA publishes "Supply of Black Soil, Red Soil (Sieved) & Murrum (Sieved) for Ground B and Practice Pitches" and a separate "Supply of Black Soil at International Cricket Stadium, New Chandigarh (Mullanpur)"; Jharkhand CA published "Supply of fine textured black cotton clay suitable for construction of cricket pitches for J.S.C.A. International Cricket Stadium Complex" (07-05-2024) and "Supply of Pitch Soil for Cricket Ground and Outfield at various venues of Jharkhand for BCCI Domestic Matches 2025–26" (25-10-2025, plus a corrigendum 03-11-2025). Same feed also carries the adjacent regime-changers: SISGrass hybrid stitching (Dharamshala was India's first, with Mumbai and Ahmedabad flagged next) and SubAir/sub-surface drainage installs (Chinnaswamy, ~INR 4.25 crore, 4km of pipe).
- **How to collect it:** Three tiers, all free and sanctioned (public procurement notices, institutional publishers only):
(1) Weekly crawl + PDF-diff of the ~35 state association tender pages. Confirmed live: cricketpunjab.in/tenders_archives.php (PCA), cricjharkhand.org/tenders/ (JSCA), ksca.cricket/tender-notifications/ (KSCA, 403s to naive fetch — needs a real browser UA/headless). Add MCA, TNCA, HCA, DDCA, CAB, GCA, HPCA, RCA, MPCA, etc. Watch the association's own social/press feeds as the corroboration channel.
(2) Government e-procurement, for the many stadiums owned by state sports departments/PWD rather than the association (Green Park Kanpur, Barabati Cuttack, Ekana Lucknow): CPPP at eprocure.gov.in plus state portals (mahatenders.gov.in, etenders.up.nic.in, tender.telangana.gov.in, eproc.rajasthan.gov.in) and GeM (gem.gov.in). Standing keyword set: "pitch soil", "black cotton", "red soil", "murrum", "cricket pitch", "square", "outfield", "turf", "Bermuda", "sub-surface drainage", "sub-air".
(3) Rest of the subcontinent: Pakistan's PPRA/EPADS at epms.ppra.gov.pk/public/tenders/active-tenders (keyword-searchable; PCB confirmed as a listed procuring organization, org page ppra.org.pk/dad_org.asp?orgid=308) and Bangladesh e-GP at eprocure.gov.bd. Vendor-side confirmation from sispitches.com press releases (hybrid installs), SubAir, and Pakistan's Nandipur Special Pitch Clay.
Backstop aggregators if you want coverage before your own crawler is complete: tenderdetail.com (Cricket Association, agid=4787) and tatanexarc. Total build: one determined scraper engineer, ~3 weeks, then ~2 hrs/week of human triage on the diffs.
- **The bet / trade:** Two expressions off one signal, keyed to the direction the soil class implies (red soil/murrum = sandier, faster-draining, pace and carry, breaks up late; black cotton = smectitic, high-clay, holds moisture, grips and slows; hybrid stitch = even bounce, suppressed deterioration).

SHORT-LEAD (the core): For the FIRST 2–4 professionally-priced matches on the new square, bet the pre-match totals lines that are set off venue history —
• T20/ODI: "1st Innings Runs" Over/Under and team-total lines (Bet365, Betfair exchange, Asian books). Over on a red-soil/murrum relay or a hybrid stitch; Under on a black-cotton relay with a low grass cover. Same direction on match total sixes/fours.
• Tests: black-cotton relay → lay the Draw, back Under on match aggregate runs, back "match does not reach Day 5"; red-soil or hybrid relay → back the Draw, back Over.
• Drainage/SubAir install → back "match completed"/lay "No Result–Abandoned", back Over on total overs bowled, for that venue's monsoon-window fixtures. Washout priors at a venue are pure history and a vacuum drainage system falsifies them overnight.
Settlement: each match, so 1 day to 5 days.

LONG-LEAD (where the months of notice actually monetize): Betfair outrights that trade 1–6 months ahead and are explicitly priced off stale venue models — Series Correct Score, Series Winner, and above all Top Series Wicket-Taker (take the seamer over the market-favoured spinner when the relay is red-soil/imported-clay, and vice versa). Hold to series end.
- **Mechanism:** Pitch behaviour is a soil-mineralogy problem, not a vibes problem, and the trade is buying the input spec before anyone observes the output. BCCI/first-class specification calls for roughly 28–35% clay; within that band the mineralogy does the work. High-clay smectitic black cotton soil shrinks and swells, binds moisture, and cracks into the seams that grip a finger-spinner's ball — slow, low, turning, low first-innings totals, results inside four days. Red soil and murrum carry more sand and less clay, drain and dry faster, hold true bounce longer under a light live-grass binder, and only break up late — pace and carry, higher totals, more boundaries, more draws in Tests. A hybrid stitch injects polymer fibre into the turf specifically to resist the stresses that create footmarks and deterioration, which structurally kills the "Day 4 collapse" that every historical model at that venue has priced in. Sub-air vacuum drainage clears an outfield roughly 36x faster and restarts play in 15–20 minutes, collapsing the abandonment probability the book carries for that ground.

So: a tender for a soil-class change is a leading indicator that the venue's entire statistical identity is about to reset. The observed magnitude is not subtle — Arun Jaitley Stadium's relaid strip produced a first-innings average of about 213 against a historical venue average of about 169, roughly +26%, and flipped bat-first outcomes to 7 of 10. The first 2–3 matches on any new square are where the anomalies cluster, and that is precisely the window in which every price in the market is still being generated from the old square's data.
- **Who's on the other side:** The people whose entire pricing input is the thing this signal invalidates.
• Bookmaker cricket traders and the model shops feeding them run a venue parameter that is a multi-year rolling average built from Cricsheet / ESPNcricinfo ball-by-ball history. That parameter is structurally incapable of knowing the square underneath it is new. They do not update it until results force them to — meaning they update AFTER the window closes.
• The analytics vendors (CricViz, Opta, Sportradar) sell ball-tracking and historical venue models. Their product is the stale prior. They have no procurement feed and no commercial reason to build one.
• The enormous Indian/Pakistani "pitch report" tipster ecosystem publishes venue historical averages as the prediction — I pulled several of these pages and they are literally recycled multi-year venue stats. Recreational money follows them, so the public flow pushes the line the wrong way, which is what pays you.
• On-the-ground syndicates are the one genuinely informed counterparty — they do get curator intel, but typically 24–72 hours pre-match, and it concerns the specific strip's rolling, watering and grass cut, not the structural soil-class change made in the off-season. They also cannot express a months-ahead view in outright markets.
• The associations themselves have zero incentive to publicise any of it. The tender exists because BCCI and government procurement rules compel disclosure, not because anyone is meant to read it. It is a compliance byproduct that happens to be alpha.
- **Information lead:** Genuinely large, and it is two separate leads.
Against the media/curator news cycle: 3–9 months. The JSCA black-cotton clay tender went up 07-05-2024 for a square that would be played on in the following domestic season; the Jharkhand pitch-soil tender for "BCCI Domestic Matches 2025-26" was published 25-10-2025 with a corrigendum 03-11-2025. The first English-language mention that a venue "looks different" arrives in a pitch report the morning of the match.
Against the market's venue prior actually converging: you are ahead by the entire first 2–4 matches on the new square. Books only reweight a venue parameter once results accumulate, so the mispricing does not close on the tender date, on the relay date, or even at first bounce — it closes empirically, over several matches. On Arun Jaitley that lag was worth roughly 44 runs of first-innings expectation per match while it persisted.
Against the sharpest on-site syndicate: you are roughly level at T-48 hours, but you had the structural information at T-6 months, which is the only reason the outright expression exists at all. Nobody else in the market can take a Top Series Wicket-Taker position in January on the basis of what soil a board bought in October.
- **Why still under-exploited:** It is fragmented, unglamorous, low-frequency, and in the wrong languages — the four things that reliably keep quant sports money away.
• There is no aggregation and no API. The data is scattered across ~35 independent association websites (several of which 403 a naive scraper), multiple state e-procurement portals, GeM, PPRA/EPADS in Pakistan and e-GP in Bangladesh. Much of it is scanned PDF; annexures and soil specs are often in Hindi or a regional language. Assembling it is manual, ugly work that does not look like a data-science project.
• Nobody sells it. There is no Kpler-for-cricket-grounds. The commercial sports-data industry sells ball-tracking, historical venue models and live odds feeds — i.e. it sells the very prior this signal falsifies, and has an incentive not to undermine it.
• It fires maybe 12–25 times a year across the whole region. Quant shops optimise for high-frequency machine-readable feeds and structurally ignore a signal that needs human triage and pays out in lumps.
• The betting-analytics community in cricket has converged hard on ball-by-ball modelling and on toss/dew, which is the hinted-at, thoroughly-crowded stuff. Everyone is modelling how the pitch behaves. Almost no one is reading the purchase order for what the pitch is made of.
Note also the regime is expanding, not shrinking: SISGrass is investing in India for a "next phase" of hybrid installs with Mumbai and Ahmedabad flagged, and Pakistan has been importing Australian soil and cycling foreign chief curators (Hemming in 2024, out 2025) — so the rate of venue-identity resets is rising while the market's method for detecting them has not changed.
- **Rough +EV:** Frequency: ~30–45 BCCI-accredited venues plus ~8 Pakistan and ~8 Bangladesh/Sri Lanka. Full or partial square relays, hybrid stitches, outfield re-turfs and drainage installs realistically produce 12–25 actionable venue-regime changes per year, each carrying 3–8 professionally-priced matches inside the stale window (IPL, Ranji, SMAT, PSL, BPL, internationals). Call it 60–150 bettable events a year, plus 3–8 outright positions.
Edge: the observed structural shift is large — +26% on first-innings expectation at Arun Jaitley, ~44 runs against a line. You will not capture that cleanly, because partial relays (often only 2–4 of 10–12 strips), curator rolling/watering regimes, and grass cover all dilute it. Conditional on a confirmed soil-class change, I'd underwrite 6–12% edge on first-innings totals at 1.90 for the first two matches, decaying to 2–4% by match four. Blended ~4–7% ROI on turnover. The drainage/abandonment leg is smaller but much cleaner: rare, but a near-binary repricing when a monsoon-window fixture comes up at a newly SubAir'd ground.
The binding constraint is limits, not edge. Indian domestic markets are thin; the deep-limit events (IPL, internationals, PSL) are a minority of the relays. Realistic capacity ~$150k–$600k of annual turnover at 4–7%, so roughly $10k–$40k/yr, with meaningful upside if the outright expression works and you can get size down on Betfair months ahead. This is a strong small-team edge, not a fund strategy — which is exactly why it has survived.
- **Confidence:** MEDIUM. Every link in the chain is verified: the tenders exist and name soil classes explicitly (PCA, JSCA confirmed live), the soil-mineralogy-to-pitch-behaviour mechanism is documented and codified in BCCI's own 28–35% clay spec, and the market impact is measurable (Arun Jaitley +26% first-innings after relay).

Biggest uncertainty: ATTRIBUTION AND EXECUTION LAG between tender and turf. A published tender is a purchase intent, not a relaid square. Tenders get cancelled, re-issued (note JSCA's corrigendum), scoped down, or fulfilled for practice pitches and Ground B rather than the centre square — the PCA notice literally says "Ground B and Practice Pitches," which would be a total false positive if traded naively. And even after a genuine relay, the curator's grass cut, rolling schedule and watering on match morning can swamp the soil-class effect for any single game. This makes a confirmation layer mandatory rather than optional: vernacular local press, association social feeds, curator interviews, vendor press releases, and ideally a human eyeball on the square — and it means you should size to the second match, not the first.

Secondary uncertainty: whether the sharpest Asian books already absorb this via curator contacts within 24 hours of the toss. If they do, the short-lead totals edge compresses substantially and the strategy's value concentrates almost entirely in the months-ahead outright markets, where the procurement lead is unarguable and unmatched.

---
## 36. The November 16 Exception

**Arena:** gambling — esports match betting (LoL/VALORANT match-winner, map handicap, correct score) at Pinnacle/bet365/Betway/GG.bet, plus split & Worlds outrights and Polymarket LoL markets
**Domain:** Esports & competitive gaming. RULES: institutions/publishers only, never individual player surveillance; sanctioned collection

- **Data source:** Riot Games' **Global Contract Database (GCD)** — the actual league-operations player registry Riot publishes on its Competitive Operations portal (competitiveops.riotgames.com, launched Aug 2025). It is a live Google Sheet, ID `1Y7k5kQ2AegbuyiGwEPsa62e883FYVtHqr6UVut9RC4o`, with six tabs (INFO / AME / CN / EMEA / KR / APAC). I pulled and parsed it: EMEA 437 people, KR 160, APAC 344 (AME and CN not pulled). Columns: League, Team, Official Summoner Name, Role, Legal First Name, Legal Family Name, **Contract End Date (Month, Day, Year) at 23:59 UTC**, Import/Residency status, LTR status, team contact email — plus a per-tab cell "THIS SHEET WAS LAST UPDATED ON: 8/5/2026 2:52:36" (EMEA), "Jul 13, 2026 18:44:24 PT" (KR), "8/3/2026" (APAC). Coverage is not just the franchise leagues (LEC 139 rows, LCK 160, LCP 72) — it includes every tier-2 league books still price: LFL 85, PRM 85, TCL 57, LES 54, SL 7, LJL 113, VCS 76, PCS 65, OPQ 6. A parallel VCT/VALORANT GCD exists in the same form. Riot publishes **only current state, no version history**. Companion feed: the Rulings repository at competitiveops.riotgames.com/en-US/rulings (35 entries 2018–2026, incl. "Gambling", "Match-fixing or Manipulation", suspensions in EMEA/APAC semi-pro), whose Next.js RSC payload embeds a JSON array with `_createdAt` and `displayedPublishDate` per ruling.
- **How to collect it:** Verified working, no auth, sanctioned public docs. The `/export?format=csv` and `/gviz/tq` endpoints return **401** (the sheet is "published to web," not link-shared) — that alone is why nobody scrapes it. The working endpoint is the published-HTML tab renderer: `https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1Y7k5kQ2AegbuyiGwEPsa62e883FYVtHqr6UVut9RC4o/pubhtml/sheet?headers=false&gid={0|594163931|148326031|905624073|1177719586}` → HTTP 200, ~420–490 KB of plain `<tr>/<td>` per tab. Parse with a 20-line regex/BeautifulSoup pass, key rows on (league, team, summoner_name), and write an **append-only diff log** — that log is the entire asset, because Riot keeps none. Cheap change detection: read the "LAST UPDATED ON" cell first; only full-diff when it moves. Poll every 5–15 min (~2 MB/poll, trivially within normal browsing volume). Add the rulings feed by fetching `https://competitiveops.riotgames.com/en-US/rulings` (HTTP 200, 97 KB) and pulling the embedded document array out of the `self.__next_f.push` flight payload. Collection touches only publisher-published institutional registry documents — no player accounts, no tracking of individuals.
- **The bet / trade:** **Leg 1 (scheduled, the main one).** From the registry, build a calendar of every registered player whose contract end date is NOT Riot's standard Nov 15/16 boundary and falls inside the season. On/after that date, fade that player's team: back the opponent on match-winner and on the **+1.5 map handicap / opponent 2-0 correct score** in the team's next 1–3 matches. Settlement horizon: the match (hours to days). Concentrate stakes in LFL / PRM / TCL / LES / LJL / VCS / PCS, where books post lines but carry no roster desk.
**Leg 2 (event-driven).** When a diff shows a date mutating, a row deleted, or a new row appearing mid-season, take the same match-level position in the implied direction on the next posted line.
**Leg 3 (futures).** Through the Nov 17–Jan transfer window, GCD diffs precede announcements by construction; hit stale next-split outrights, Worlds/VCT qualification futures, and Polymarket LoL markets. Horizon: weeks to months, or exit on the announcement pop.
- **Mechanism:** A player cannot be fielded in a Riot league unless he sits on the league's registered roster under an unexpired contract — and the GCD *is* that registry, not a fan wiki (the sheet literally carries team ops contact emails). Riot standardizes nearly every contract to the same expiry: 252 of 317 dated EMEA rows read "November 16, 2026"; 126 of 160 LCK rows read the same. **Every non-standard date is therefore a deliberate, league-approved short deal** — a loan, a mid-season out clause, a stand-in agreement, or a buyout already papered — and it publishes, in advance, the exact date on which that roster is mechanically forced to change. Roster composition is the single largest driver of an esports price: swapping a starter for an academy sub is worth roughly 5–15 percentage points of true win probability, far more than any form or meta variable a book models. So the registry hands you a dated, publisher-certified schedule of forced lineup changes. Live as of today (Aug 14, 2026), from the EMEA tab last written Aug 5: **LEC starter Natus Vincere top laner "Empyros" carries a registered contract end of August 4, 2026 — already lapsed, mid-Summer-Split.** The entire ZYB Esport LFL roster (Adam, Manaty, SAKEN, Jezu, Riippp + two subs) expires Sept 16, 2026. Falke Esports is split across Aug 15 and Sept 30; LUA's whole starting five plus coach ends Sept 30; Elche/ES's mid Fantomisto Sept 30 with its coach out Sept 1. Those are six-plus roster detonations with dates attached, published, and unpriced.
- **Who's on the other side:** Bookmakers' esports traders and the three vendors that actually feed them — PandaScore, Abios/Sportradar, and Bayes/GRID. Their roster tables are keyed to *announcements*: team social posts, Leaguepedia/Liquipedia edits, and the broadcast lineup graphic. None of them ingest a Riot compliance Google Sheet, and critically **none of them carry a contract-end-date field at all**, so they structurally cannot anticipate a lapse — they can only react to it after the fact. On the other side of the retail flow: bettors who read English esports media, which covers LEC/LCK headline moves and near-totally ignores LFL, PRM, TCL, LES, LJL, VCS, PCS and OPQ — leagues every major book nonetheless prices daily. The one adjacent competitor, @ScanLeague on X ("LoL Contract Updates"), does watch GCD changes, but it is LoL-only, human-latency, publishes to fans and journalists rather than trading, and polling the sheet directly beats it by minutes to hours.
- **Information lead:** Two distinct and independent leads. **(1) Scheduled — weeks.** The September 16 and September 30, 2026 expiry cohort is readable today, August 14: that is 4–7 weeks of advance notice on a forced lineup change the bookmaker will learn about when the draft screen loads. This leg does not depend on Riot's write cadence at all; the date is already published. **(2) Event-driven — hours to days.** When a date field mutates or a row appears/disappears, that write lands the moment Riot league ops approves the paperwork, which is strictly upstream of the org's PR. The community consensus during the transfer window is explicit — "a signing isn't real until it's in the GCD" — i.e. the sheet is by construction ahead of the announcement, which is itself ahead of the vendor feed, which is ahead of the line. Realistic capture: 6–72 hours event-driven, 2–7 weeks scheduled. Bonus lead from the paired rulings feed: entry timestamps show Riot creating the record hours before publishing it (Jakub "Syzyf" Ruta, gambling violation — `_createdAt` 2026-07-23T08:53:13Z vs. displayed publish 2026-07-23T16:30:00Z, a 7.6-hour embargo gap).
- **Why still under-exploited:** Four compounding reasons. (a) **It fails the easy scrape.** The CSV and gviz endpoints 401; only the pubhtml table renderer works, so anyone who tried the obvious path concluded the sheet was locked. (b) **Riot keeps no version history** — only current state. The diff log therefore cannot be bought, backfilled, or copied; it exists only for whoever started snapshotting, and no public longitudinal GCD dataset exists today. The asset compounds solely for its owner. (c) **The portal is brand new** — competitiveops.riotgames.com only launched in August 2025, consolidating what were previously scattered per-region blog posts. (d) **Audience mismatch.** The people who read the GCD are roster journalists and fantasy players; the people who bet esports do not read Riot's compliance portal. And the commercial esports alt-data industry sells match telemetry and announced rosters — "league registry diffs and contract-expiry calendars" is simply not a product category anyone packages. Nothing here resembles a Kpler/Ortex-style vendor feed; it is a spreadsheet with a broken export button on a compliance microsite.
- **Rough +EV:** Firing rate: in the EMEA tab alone I count ~28 live non-standard in-season expiry dates right now; extrapolating across AME, CN, KR, APAC plus the VCT GCD gives roughly 60–150 actionable, line-moving events per year once tier-2 leagues are included, clustered around split boundaries and the Nov–Jan window. Edge per event: the forced swap is worth ~5–15 pp of true win probability; realistically you capture 2–6 pp before the market corrects, i.e. **~3–6% ROI per bet**, higher on the ERLs where no roster desk exists at all. The binding constraint is stake limits, not edge: $200–$2,000 per side on LEC/LCK at Pinnacle/bet365, $50–$500 on LFL/PRM/LES. That puts a realistic one-person operation at roughly **$8k–$40k/yr of +EV on match markets**, plus lumpier and less capacity-constrained futures during the November transfer window. Honest framing: this is a $50k/yr edge, not a $5M/yr one — and that capacity ceiling is precisely why it survives. Too small for a syndicate to build, too obscure for a vendor to package.
- **Confidence:** **Medium.** Everything structural is verified first-hand, not inferred: the sheets are live, public, machine-readable, per-tab timestamped, carry contract end dates, and cover exactly the tier-2 leagues books price. **Biggest uncertainty: Riot's write cadence versus announcement timing.** The KR tab was last written Jul 13, EMEA Aug 5, APAC Aug 3 — Riot appears to batch edits per region rather than writing per-transaction, which means the event-driven leg (Leg 2) may collapse to near-zero lead on high-profile LEC/LCK moves the org tweets the same morning ops approves them. The scheduled-expiry leg (Leg 1) is immune to this, does not depend on cadence, and is where I would put the weight; Leg 2 needs 2–3 months of snapshotting to measure the true registry→announcement gap distribution before sizing up. Secondary, underrated risk: many esports books reserve the right to **void** bets when a lineup change is announced after the bet is struck — confirm each book's roster-change void rule in writing before this is bankable, or the edge is real and uncollectable.

---
## 37. The Carding Residual (Silent Decard)

**Arena:** gambling — Olympic/World Championship outright, medal and country-total markets (Betfair/Smarkets ante-post + ante-post bookmakers)
**Domain:** Olympic, championship & niche event markets. RULES: institutions only; sanctioned collection

- **Data source:** National high-performance institutes' and sport ministries' PUBLISHED PER-ATHLETE FUNDING-TIER REGISTERS, and specifically the year-over-year *residual* between the tier an athlete actually receives and the tier their public results mechanically earn. These are official public-spending records, published by name and tier, on fixed annual cycles, in ~15 countries — and nobody assembles or diffs them. Verified live examples: (1) UK — British Athletics/UKA publishes the complete named 2025/26 World Class Programme list split into Podium / Podium Potential / Confirmation (21 Olympic Podium, 14 Podium Potential, 15 Relay, 10 Confirmation), explicitly defined as athletes with "realistic potential and demonstrable performance capability to achieve podium success at the 2027 World Championships in Beijing, the 2028 Olympic Games in Los Angeles"; the matching UKA WCP Selection Policy PDF sets out a three-stage process (eligibility criteria → Performance Matrix → panel selects "the athletes it considers most likely to achieve a medal"), i.e. an explicit, documented discretionary layer. (2) Finland — Opetus- ja kulttuuriministeriö publishes the named summer/winter urheilija-apuraha decisions: for 2026, 76 summer-sport athletes, 26 at EUR 24,000 (19 of them for TWO years, 7 for one) and 50 at EUR 12,000; the one-vs-two-year split at identical money is a pure confidence signal, and the sport-level reallocation is visible too (Finnish media headline: athletics support collapsed). (3) Brazil — Ministério do Esporte portarias in the Diário Oficial da União list all 10,885 Bolsa Atleta recipients for 2026 by name, modality and tier (463 Olímpico/Paralímpico at R$3,437; 1,668 Internacional; 7,058 Nacional), plus the separate elite Bolsa Pódio list. (4) Netherlands — each bond publishes a dated, versioned "Lijst atleten met NOC*NSF-status" PDF (A-status / HP / Bondsstatus / Belofte), e.g. Watersportverbond "2026 Versie 28-04-26", KNAF "per 1 januari 2026" — versioned means intra-season diffable. (5) Ireland — Sport Ireland publishes the International Carding Scheme Criteria and Guidelines PDFs plus the annual carded list (139 individually funded athletes in 2026). (6) Canada — Sport Canada AAP with each NSO's published carding criteria (e.g. Tennis Canada's "2027 Sport Canada Carding Criteria"). Extendable: AIS dAIS (AUS), SOK Topp och Talang (SWE), Team Danmark categorisation, Poland/Hungary ministry sports-stipend decisions, and Italy's Gruppi Sportivi Militari recruitment rankings published in the Gazzetta Ufficiale.
- **How to collect it:** All public, no login, robots-respecting — a monthly crawler over ~40 fixed URLs plus two gazette APIs. Concretely: britishathletics.org.uk annual "World Class Programme Athletes Announced" posts and uka.org.uk/wp-content/uploads/ selection-policy PDFs (versioned with publication dates); okm.fi + valtioneuvosto.fi decision pages "Kesälajien/Talvilajien urheilija-apurahat myönnetty" with the recipient-and-amount annex; Brazil's in.gov.br (Diário Oficial da União) full-text search API for Ministério do Esporte "Bolsa Atleta"/"Bolsa Pódio" portarias, plus the gov.br contemplados spreadsheets; each Dutch bond's status PDF (watersportverbond.nl, knaf.nl, atletiekunie.nl, knzb.nl, knwu.nl); sportireland.ie carding criteria/guidelines PDFs; Sport Canada AAP page plus each NSO's carding-criteria PDF. Pipeline: fetch → pdftotext/table extraction → fuzzy-match names to World Athletics / World Aquatics / UCI / FISA athlete IDs → store a DATED SNAPSHOT every month (critical: the informative event is a name that is *absent* this year, which is unrecoverable unless you kept last year's file) → parse the published mechanical criteria (top-8 at Worlds, top-16 ranking, performance standard) and compute each athlete's "earned" tier from public results → residual = actual tier − earned tier. Cost: one person, a few weeks to build, then ~2 hours/month. Fully sanctioned: these are official records of public expenditure, published for transparency.
- **The bet / trade:** Two legs, both settling at a named championship, 6–24 month hold. (A) FADE THE SILENT DECARD: when an athlete is dropped or demoted from their institute's top tier (UKA Podium → Podium Potential/off; NOC*NSF A-status → Bondsstatus; Finnish EUR 24k → EUR 12k or off; Bolsa Pódio → Bolsa Atleta) despite public results that mechanically qualified them, LAY that athlete on Betfair in the outright-winner and "to win a medal" markets for the next global championship — currently 2027 World Athletics Championships (Beijing) and LA 2028 — and recycle the stake into the two or three rivals whose implied probability must rise. (B) BACK THE DISCRETIONARY UPGRADE: when an athlete is promoted into the top tier (or given Finland's two-year rather than one-year EUR 24k award) WITHOUT the public results to earn it, back them ante-post in outright/medal and "to reach the final" markets. (C) AGGREGATE OVERLAY: use per-country, per-sport tier counts year-over-year to trade country medal-total and "top nation in athletics/swimming" over/unders (e.g. a visible collapse in Finnish athletics funding for 2026 → sell FIN athletics medal props for 2027/2028). Entry window: the northern autumn/winter, when the lists drop. Exit: hold to settlement, or take the mid-season price move once the athlete's season opener confirms the signal.
- **Mechanism:** A national institute must irrevocably commit real money to named athletes by a fixed autumn deadline, and it does so holding information the public and the book do not have: physiological lab testing, training-load and power/GPS data, rehab status of an injury that was never announced, whether the athlete has changed or lost a coach, relocated, enrolled in a degree, become pregnant, or privately signalled they are winding down, plus the head coach's honest read of the athlete's trajectory. The published tier is a costly, legally-documented public signal of that private assessment. UKA's own policy makes the channel explicit: after the objective eligibility screen and the Performance Matrix, a panel picks "the athletes it considers most likely to achieve a medal" — that final step is pure insider judgement, and it is published. Crucially, the *mechanical* component (top-8 at Worlds, top-16 ranking, a performance standard) is already fully in the market price, because it is just last season's results. So the alpha is the residual: the athlete who cleared the standard and still got cut, and the athlete who missed the standard and got Podium anyway. Empirically that residual resolves 6–18 months later as a withdrawal, an injury announcement, a quiet retirement, a form collapse — or a breakout. The negative residual is the stronger of the two, because dropping a decorated athlete is politically expensive and embarrassing for an institute; they only do it when they are quite sure.
- **Who's on the other side:** Ante-post compilers at bookmakers, who price Olympic and World Championship markets almost entirely off world rankings, season's bests and the previous championship's result, and recreational ante-post money, which is name-driven and buys the famous athlete. On the exchange, the counterparty is a small pool of ante-post traders who react to news — press releases, injury reports, race results — not to administrative registers. They are slow for structural reasons: the informative event is a NON-EVENT. There is no press release saying "athlete X lost Podium funding." Their name is simply not in this year's PDF, which means you can only see it if you archived last year's PDF. The publications are also deliberately unglamorous and multilingual: a Finnish ministry decision annex, a Brazilian portaria buried in the Diário Oficial, a Dutch sailing federation's versioned status list, a UKA WordPress upload. Sports desks cover the headline funding total and the one famous casualty; nobody reconciles 2,000 names across 15 jurisdictions. And the sports involved (athletics, swimming, rowing, canoe, track cycling, sailing) are too small for any quant fund to bother, while sports-betting syndicates concentrate on football, tennis, basketball and horses where the money is.
- **Information lead:** Publication is 8–11 months before the following summer's global championship and 20–30 months before an Olympics: UKA drops the WCP list in Sept/Oct for the coming season; Finland's OKM decides in late autumn for the next calendar year (and now for two years); Dutch bond status lists carry a 1 January effective date and are revised mid-season with a version date; Brazil's portaria lands in March. The market's first observable signal for the same athlete is either their season opener (April–June) or an injury/withdrawal announcement — typically 4 to 10 months later. For the Olympic ante-post book the lead is larger still: the 2025/26 UKA list is explicitly a judgement about LA 2028, published roughly two and a half years before settlement, into markets that are already quoted. Even the aggregate country-level signal (which sports had funding cut) leads the medal-total market by a full cycle. Realistic, honest lead: 4–10 months for the single-championship trade, up to ~24 months for the Olympic trade, and effectively infinite for the "absence" cases that are never reported at all.
- **Why still under-exploited:** No product exists for this, and the structural reasons it doesn't are the reasons it stays exploitable. (1) Fragmentation: ~15 national systems with different fiscal years, tier vocabularies (Podium/A-status/carded SR1/apuraha/Bolsa Pódio), languages and publication venues — an institute press release in one country, a ministry decision in another, an official gazette in a third, a federation PDF in a fourth. (2) The key signal is an absence, so the dataset does not exist unless you built the time series yourself; there is no archive to buy. Nobody has a five-year panel of who was quietly decarded. (3) The residual requires you to also parse each system's published mechanical criteria to compute what an athlete "should" have received — a second, equally unglamorous corpus. (4) The affected markets are small, so the alt-data industry (Kpler, YipitData, M Science, Ortex and friends) has zero interest; sports-betting data vendors sell odds feeds, injury feeds and xG, not government funding gazettes. (5) The one place this data does get read — inside the institutes and by the athletes' agents — is precisely the place that cannot legally or reputationally trade on it. What coverage exists is a single-country, single-year news story about the headline total, never a longitudinal cross-national panel.
- **Rough +EV:** Tracking universe: roughly 2,000–3,000 athletes across the ~12 most transparent funding systems and the 8–10 Olympic sports with real ante-post markets. Expected ~150–250 "off-model" tier events per year; after requiring a live, quoted market with any liquidity, ~30–60 tradeable situations annually. Fade leg (silent decard): typical target is priced ante-post around 4.0–8.0 to medal; I'd expect true probability to sit roughly 6–10 percentage points below implied, i.e. a 7–12% edge per bet, and I'd expect roughly 55–60% of these athletes to underperform their ante-post-implied outcome versus a ~45% base rate. Back leg (discretionary upgrade): thinner, ~3–7% edge, on longer prices (15.0–60.0) so variance is high and it is really a lottery-ticket book. Capacity is the binding constraint, not edge: ante-post exchange liquidity on a niche athletics medal market is roughly GBP 100–2,000, and bookmaker limits on obscure athletes are GBP 20–200. Realistic: GBP 30,000–60,000 turned over per year at a 6–9% blended edge, i.e. GBP 2,000–5,000 net after 2–5% commission — a genuine but small standalone business. It scales meaningfully in one direction only: fold the residual in as a feature in a championship medal model and deploy it during the Games fortnight itself, when Olympic athletics and swimming outrights carry six-figure matched volume and the signal is still, remarkably, unpriced. The country medal-total overlay is the other capacity outlet, since those markets take five-figure stakes.
- **Confidence:** Medium. Biggest uncertainty by far: what fraction of tier assignment is genuinely private information versus a mechanical lag on public results. If institutes largely rubber-stamp their published criteria, the residual is near zero and the market already has everything. The evidence cuts in my favour — UKA's own policy documents an explicit discretionary panel stage after the Performance Matrix, and Finland's new one-year-versus-two-year split at identical money is a confidence judgement that no formula produces — but the discretionary share varies enormously by country (Canada's AAP carding is close to purely mechanical, so it should probably be dropped from the panel; UKA, Finland, and Dutch A-status are heavily discretionary). This needs a 3–5 year backtest of residuals against subsequent championship outcomes before sizing up, and that backtest is itself hard because historical snapshots of the "absence" side may have to be reconstructed from the Wayback Machine and gazette archives. Two secondary risks: reverse causality on the fade leg (losing funding can itself cause the decline, which is fine for the trade but means the signal partly reflects the institute's power rather than its knowledge, and could weaken if funding rules change), and ante-post void/dead-heat rules — confirm each book's treatment of non-starters before laying, since a voided market kills the trade you actually wanted.

---
## 38. The NomCom Reel

**Arena:** gambling — regulated/offshore awards event contracts (Polymarket, Kalshi), plus UK/Irish awards books
**Domain:** Awards & entertainment prediction markets. RULES: institutions only; sanctioned collection

- **Data source:** The raw HTML of studio/distributor "For Your Consideration" microsites — and, more valuably, the public unauthenticated S3/CDN asset keys those pages link to. Two layers nobody parses: (1) each film's page carries the studio's exhaustive campaign slate as machine-readable category–name pairs broken out by voting body. A24's Marty Supreme page alone carries 281 "Best …" lines spanning Academy Awards, BAFTA, SAG, PGA, DGA, WGA, Golden Globes, Critics Choice, NBR, MUAHS, AARP, Dorian — including the exact placement decision ("Best Actor in a Leading Role – Timothée Chalamet") and every craft name ("Best Casting – Jennifer Venditti", "Best Costume Design – Miyako Bellizzi"), plus vote-split tells like two names run simultaneously in Supporting Actress (Gwyneth Paltrow AND Odessa A'zion). (2) The linked video assets sit on an open S3 bucket at s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/files.a24screenings.com/video/<slug>/ and the object keys encode the target electorate and event date: MARTY_SUPREME_PANEL_111325_SAG_NOMCOM_QnA_FINAL.mp4 (SAG Nominating Committee panel, Nov 13 2025), MartySupreme_Panel_QnA-SAG-NYC_Final.mp4, MARTY_SUPREME_PANEL_111425_FYC_QnA_FINAL.mp4. A HEAD on any key returns an exact staging timestamp (Last-Modified: Mon, 24 Nov 2025 21:14:06 GMT). A third layer: unauthenticated audience-segmentation params — a24awards.com/?pga=true sets a cookie (hide-nav-films.js) that strips the nav to a PGA-voter-specific title set, so you can read what each guild's electorate is actually being shown.
- **How to collect it:** Seed list is maintained free every season by AwardsWatch ("Studio FYC Pages for 2025/2026 Film Awards Season"): a24awards.com, film.netflixawards.com, awards.universalpictures.com, searchlightpictures.com/awards, disneystudiosawards.com, focusfeaturesawards.com, wbawards.com, sonypictures-awards.com, sonyclassicsawards.com, neonratedawards.com, paramountpicturesfyc.com, lionsgateawards.com, amazonmgmstudiosguilds.com, fyc.appletvplus.com, ifcfilmsawards.com, magnoliapictures.com/awards. Pipeline: (a) nightly GET of each domain root, extract flat per-film slugs (a24awards.com/marty-supreme, /the-smashing-machine, /eddington …) — these sites serve cache-control: no-cache, no-store, so every fetch is fresh; (b) regex/DOM-parse the slate into (award_body, category, person, film) tuples and store as a daily snapshot; (c) diff snapshots for placement flips, name adds/drops, award-body adds/drops; (d) harvest every S3/CDN asset key from the HTML and HEAD each one for Last-Modified and Content-Length — ListBucket is AccessDenied, so keys must come from the crawl, which is exactly why this is unexploited; (e) fuzz known audience params (?pga=true and siblings) to diff guild-segmented views. All public, unauthenticated, no login, no paywall, HEAD-only against S3, polite rate limits, no screener content downloaded or redistributed. Build cost: roughly one engineer-week of per-studio scrapers, rebuilt each September because the microsites are rebuilt each season.
- **The bet / trade:** Polymarket/Kalshi Oscar acting and screenplay contracts (winner and nomination), plus SAG Awards and Golden Globes nomination markets. Three trades. (1) PLACEMENT FLIP — when the slate diff shows a name move between Lead and Supporting (or a screenplay move Original↔Adapted), buy the destination-category contract at 2–6c and sell/short the origin contract. Live case as of Aug 2026: Universal has not settled Anne Hathaway's placement for The Odyssey; Gold Derby experts have her #1 in Supporting Actress near 90% and the books mirror that. The night awards.universalpictures.com publishes her under "Actress in a Leading Role," her Supporting contract goes to zero and Lead reprices. (2) NOMCOM ACCESS — buy nomination contracts at 2–8c on performers who appear in a newly staged SAG_NOMCOM-keyed asset before the panel is publicized; the SAG Nominating Committee is the single best Oscar acting precursor. (3) SILENT DE-ESCALATION FADE — short names still priced 5–20c whose slate line is quietly deleted, or whose film stops staging guild events after mid-November. Horizon: signals fire Sept–Jan; SAG noms Dec/Jan, Oscar noms late Jan, ceremony March. Hold 2 weeks to 5 months.
- **Mechanism:** Category placement is not an opinion, it is an administrative fact the distributor chooses and files, and it fully determines which market a performance can settle in — a Supporting Actress contract on a performer campaigned Lead is worth exactly zero regardless of the performance. The FYC microsite is the studio's own primary publication of that decision, so the slate line is ground truth, not a proxy. The staged assets work differently: Oscar acting nominations are overwhelmingly predicted by SAG nominations, SAG nominations are decided by a randomly drawn nominating committee of roughly two thousand SAG-AFTRA members, and physical/virtual access to that committee is a scarce, expensive, rationed resource. A studio that books, films, and stages a dedicated SAG NomCom panel for a specific performer has spent real money on the one electorate that gates the precursor — and it has done so based on internal tracking the market cannot see. Conversely, a name that stays on the slate but never gets guild-facing events has been abandoned internally while still trading at 15c. The S3 Last-Modified timestamp is the moment that decision became physical.
- **Who's on the other side:** Retail awards bettors and the Kalshi/Polymarket market makers who quote around them. Both price off the same three inputs: Gold Derby expert consensus (a weekly poll of pundits, explicitly the source cited for Hathaway at 90%), Variety/THR/Deadline awards columns, and Letterboxd/X sentiment. Pundits are prose writers on a weekly cadence who learn placement either from a publicist call or from eventually noticing the FYC page — after it changed. Their coverage is also brutally top-heavy: the trades babysit maybe ten names a season, while a single A24 film page carries 281 category–name pairs, so ~95% of the slate is unwatched by anyone. Market makers on these venues hedge inventory and follow the pundit tape; none of them runs a crawler against studio marketing microsites. And the professionals who would — YipitData, M Science, the alt-data shops — serve equity clients, not a $120M-per-season awards book that cannot absorb their AUM.
- **Information lead:** Varies sharply by tier, which is the point. Marquee names the trades are actively watching: hours to two days — real but thin. Mid-tier and craft names (supporting performers, screenplay, casting, editing, costume): days to three weeks, because no reporter is diffing the page and the studio has no incentive to announce. S3 asset staging: the object lands in the bucket before the page links it and often before the event is publicized at all, giving days to weeks of warning that a specific electorate is being worked. And the de-escalation signal — a name silently deleted from the slate, or a film's guild events simply stopping — is frequently never reported anywhere, ever. That leg has effectively infinite lead: the information is public, timestamped, and permanently unpriced.
- **Why still under-exploited:** Nobody sells it and it is genuinely annoying to build. These are ~20 bespoke marketing microsites rebuilt from scratch every season by different agencies, with no sitemap (a24awards.com/sitemap.xml is 404), no JSON API, no RSS, no structured feed — A24's runs a hand-rolled jQuery template that literally console-logs its own routing debug info. Cache headers are no-store, S3 ListBucket returns AccessDenied so keys are only obtainable by crawling and diffing the HTML, and several domains have broken or mismatched TLS certs that break naive scrapers. Wayback coverage of these domains is sparse and does not archive the asset URLs, so the historical diff series cannot be bought or backfilled — it must be accumulated forward, which is a real moat and a real cost. Meanwhile the audience that would use it is a retail and pundit crowd with no engineering function, and the awards markets are too small to interest anyone with a data team. The information is fully public and has been sitting in plain sight for a decade.
- **Rough +EV:** Placement flips: roughly 4–10 resolvable acting/screenplay placement decisions per season. Catching ~60% with a multi-day lead, destination contracts typically trade 2–6c pre-flip and 15–40c after, so 4–8x on winning legs. Liquidity is the binding constraint — long-tail Oscar name contracts on Polymarket absorb maybe $2k–15k before moving 3–5c, so call it $20k–60k deployable per flip. De-escalation fades: ~15–30 silently-dropped names per season still priced 5–20c; high hit rate (I'd guess 75–85%) but small absolute payout each. NomCom-access longs are the widest but least proven leg. Blended, I'd estimate +12% to +30% ROI on ~$300k–800k of season-cycled notional, i.e. roughly $50k–200k per season for a two-person team. It does not scale past that — the books are too thin — which is exactly why the edge survives.
- **Confidence:** Medium. Biggest uncertainty: only one of the three legs is on solid ground. The placement-flip leg is near-mechanical — the slate line IS the ground truth, so its predictive validity is definitional and the only open question is how many days of lead it actually buys, which I have not measured and which is probably near-zero for the handful of names the trades watch closely. The other two legs (NomCom panel presence as a nomination predictor, silent slate deletion as a fade) are untested hypotheses with no backtest available: Wayback does not archive the S3 keys or reliably snapshot these microsites, so the historical panel is unrecoverable and you would be building it forward and paying a season or two of tuition before knowing the hit rates. Secondary risks: thin books and wide spreads on long-tail contracts may eat the edge on execution, and Kalshi/Polymarket category definitions can differ from the studio's own label, creating basis risk where you are right about the placement and still wrong about settlement.

---
## 39. The August Envelope

**Arena:** Gambling / prediction markets — Polymarket ballot-measure contracts and Kalshi's CFTC-regulated event contracts on 2026 state ballot measures.
**Domain:** Political primaries, ballot measures & referenda. RULES: institutions only; sanctioned collection

- **Data source:** Parcel-level statutory assessment rolls plus the statutory assessment-notice calendar — used to compute, voter by voter, the exact dollar figure a government-mandated tax notice will print in each household's mailbox weeks before a property-tax ballot measure. Flagship: Florida DOR Property Tax Oversight NAL (Name-Address-Legal) real-property tax rolls — PRELIMINARY (July 1 certification) and final, all 67 counties, with just value, assessed value, exemption flags and prior-year values; plus the DR-420 "Certification of Taxable Value" / DR-420MM proposed-millage series filed through DOR's OASYS eTRIM in early August; plus county Value Adjustment Board (VAB) petition dockets as a real-time revealed-preference grievance meter; joined to the FL Division of Elections statewide voter file. Generalized by pre-published reappraisal calendars (NCDOR county revaluation schedule, TN Comptroller reappraisal cycle, WY/GA/OK/LA assessor rolls), which deterministically schedule which voters get a tax-shock notice in the months before a tax measure.
- **How to collect it:** 1) floridarevenue.com Property Tax Data Portal → "Download Tax Rolls and GIS Data": current preliminary and final NAL/NAP files are DIRECT free downloads per county per year; 2002-present available on request (PTOTechnology@floridarevenue.com, 850-717-6570). Field definitions in the DOR "User's Guide for Department Property Tax Data Files."
2) July 1 preliminary certifications posted directly by county appraisers (pbcpao.gov/tax-roll-news.htm, miamidadepa.gov, leepa.org); DR-420 series + proposed millages certified by taxing authorities in early August via OASYS eTRIM and in city/county budget-hearing agenda packets.
3) TRIM mailing dates announced by each property appraiser (mid-to-late August). VAB petitions are due 25 days later (~Sept 12-15); clerk portals expose filed petitions with SEQUENTIAL petition numbers — daily probe of the max number yields a running county-level count (leeclerk.org "Access Filed Petitions," vab.leonclerk.com AxiaWeb, mypalmbeachclerk.com, miamidadeclerk.gov, collierclerk.com, sarasotaclerk.com).
4) FL statewide voter file (address, party, vote history) from FL Division of Elections; address-match situs to parcel.
5) Cross-state: NCDOR's published multi-year county revaluation schedule; TN Comptroller reappraisal cycle.
Build: for every homesteaded parcel, apply the $50k existing + proposed $150k (2027) / $250k (2028) non-school exemption against assessed value × non-school millage → per-parcel dollar benefit and a binary "goes to $0"; separately flag recapture parcels (assessed value UP while just value DOWN). Aggregate to precinct, weight by midterm turnout propensity from the voter file.
- **The bet / trade:** Polymarket "Will Florida's 'Save Our Homes from Excessive Property Taxes' referendum pass in November 2026?" (and the Kalshi equivalent): BUY NO. Enter mid-August through September 2026, settle Nov 3, 2026. Sizing/flip rule: compute the turnout-weighted share of homesteaded registered voters whose non-school property tax goes to exactly $0 under the $250k exemption. Stay NO unless that share exceeds ~65% AND the county-weighted recapture share is in the top decile — then flip to YES. Run the same model as a relative-value book across the rest of the Nov 2026 property-tax slate (North Carolina's two tax-limit amendments, Tennessee's state-property-tax prohibition, Wyoming's homeowner exemption initiative, plus GA/OK/LA measures): long measures in counties/states carrying a fresh revaluation shock, short measures in stale-assessment states.
- **Mechanism:** Two mechanically computable channels run from the assessment roll to the vote. (1) Benefit incidence: Amendment 3's exemption ladder ($150k in 2027, $250k in 2028, non-school levies only) zeroes out the non-school bill for a share of homesteads that varies enormously by county — near-universal in the Panhandle and interior, a minority in Miami-Dade/Palm Beach/Monroe/Collier. Because Save Our Homes has held long-time owners' assessed values far below market, the exact "goes to zero" set is computable parcel by parcel from the NAL file and joinable to actual registered voters. No poll can measure this: respondents do not know their own assessed value or non-school millage. (2) Grievance salience: Florida's recapture rule (Rule 12D-8.0062(5)) forces a homestead's assessed value UP by the CPI/3% cap even when market value FALLS — and 2026 rolls are falling in southwest Florida (Lee County's July 1 preliminary roll is down 0.96%). That produces the maximum-anger configuration: a notice saying your house is worth less but you owe more. The TRIM notice is the delivery vehicle — a state-mandated, personalized, 100%-reach direct-mail piece landing in every Florida homeowner's box in the third/fourth week of August, roughly ten weeks before they vote on this measure. Its content is fixed by data you can already download. Against these: the 60% supermajority, the Aug 3 Leon County ruling striking the ballot title as "akin to a political slogan" and forcing an AG rewrite, and the $4.6B-to-$12B fiscal disclosure that collapsed support in both the UNF and James Madison Institute polls once tradeoffs were described.
- **Who's on the other side:** Prediction-market traders pricing off (a) statewide topline polls that sit right at the 60% line and fall decisively below it the moment budget impact is mentioned — i.e., an unstable number the market is anchored to; (b) ballot-language litigation headlines; (c) the generic "tax cuts are popular" prior and DeSantis's endorsement. Pollsters are structurally blind here: they ask about a hypothetical benefit from respondents who cannot state their own assessed value, exemption status, or non-school millage, so measured support is a symbolic response, not an incidence-weighted one. The local governments, sheriffs' and firefighters' groups funding the No campaign know the aggregate fiscal hole but have no precinct-level map of who personally goes to zero. And the 60% threshold concentrates all the market's error in the 55-65% band — precisely where parcel-level incidence is decisive and topline polling is worthless.
- **Information lead:** Preliminary parcel rolls certified July 1 and downloadable in July: ~17 weeks before Election Day. DR-420 certified values and proposed millages: early August, ~13 weeks. TRIM notices reach voters ~Aug 18-24 — so you know the exact content and geographic distribution of the persuasion shock two to six weeks BEFORE the electorate receives it. VAB petition counts readable daily from clerk dockets through the ~Sept 12-15 deadline give a revealed-preference grievance index ~7 weeks out that is aggregated nowhere (DOR publishes its statewide VAB summary only the following year). First post-TRIM public polls arrive late September/October; market repricing follows in October. Net lead over the market's information set: 6-10 weeks. For North Carolina and Tennessee the revaluation/reappraisal calendars are published years in advance, so the "which counties get shocked" variable has a multi-year lead.
- **Why still under-exploited:** Parcel assessment data is a large commercial market (ATTOM, CoreLogic, Regrid) sold exclusively into real estate, mortgage and insurance — never as an election input. Election modelers use census demographics, voter files and polling; they do not touch assessment rolls. Property-tax analysts (Lincoln Institute, Tax Foundation, state EDR) do fiscal incidence but never vote forecasting. Nobody sits in the intersection. The join is genuinely laborious and unproductized: 67 heterogeneous county NAL extracts, exemption-stacking logic, non-school millage arithmetic by overlapping taxing authority, the recapture rule, then address-matching ~10M parcels to a 14M-record voter file. VAB petition volume is not aggregated in-cycle by anyone, and the sequential-petition-number trick for reading a live count is not a known technique. Most fundamentally, treating a statutory assessment notice as a dated, pre-computable information shock delivered by the government itself to every affected voter is an idea political forecasting has simply never operationalized — even though 2026 puts property-tax measures on ballots in Florida, Georgia, Louisiana, Oklahoma, North Carolina, Tennessee and Wyoming simultaneously.
- **Rough +EV:** Base rates are the anchor: Florida's 2018 Amendment 1 — a smaller version of exactly this homestead-exemption idea — drew 58% and failed the 60% bar (the only one of twelve amendments that year to miss); 2012's Amendment 4 property-tax measure got 43%. Florida amendments polling near 60% in August systematically decay as opposition messaging lands. If the market prices YES at 45-60c against a true ~25-30% pass probability, buying NO is a 20-25 point edge on the flagship contract. Across the rest of the 2026 slate (~8 statewide tax contracts) the incidence model materially disagrees with topline polling on roughly 40% of measures, worth an estimated 5-12 points of edge on those. Fires 2-4 times a year (even-year generals, off-year specials, plus local bond/levy elections where books exist), so ~10-20 tradeable statewide contracts per two-year cycle. Hard ceiling is liquidity: ballot-measure markets clear $100k-$2M, so realistically a $50k-$500k-per-cycle strategy returning maybe 3-8% on deployed capital cycle-over-cycle — a genuine but small and non-scalable edge, with the concentrated Florida position carrying most of it.
- **Confidence:** Medium. Biggest uncertainty: whether computed personal dollar benefit actually moves votes more than partisan and symbolic cues. The self-interest voting literature is mixed-to-weak, and 2018 Amendment 1 is a direct warning shot — homesteaders who would have gained still failed to deliver 60%. The stronger half of the signal is the grievance/salience channel (recapture-driven assessment increases amid falling market values, and fresh-revaluation shocks), which has better support from the tax-salience literature and from the Prop 13 / Colorado Prop HH pattern, but it is not cleanly identified and I have no out-of-sample backtest across enough measures to size it confidently. Secondary uncertainty, currently large: the court-ordered ballot-title rewrite — the AG's revised language was due within 10 days of the Aug 3 ruling and lands right now — is an unmodeled variance term that could swamp the parcel model on the flagship trade. Practical risk: address-matching parcels to the voter file is where this quietly breaks, especially for condos and multi-unit homesteads.

---
## 40. The Fourteen-Day Ballot

**Arena:** financial-market (and prediction markets: Kalshi/Polymarket Fed contracts + CME SOFR/fed funds futures)
**Domain:** Central bank & monetary policy prediction markets. RULES: institutions only; sanctioned collection

- **Data source:** Bank-level requested primary credit rates buried in the Federal Reserve Board's "Minutes of the Board's discount rate meetings." By statute the Board must review discount rates at least every 14 days, so each of the 12 Reserve Banks' boards of directors casts a NUMERIC ballot on the primary credit rate roughly biweekly — including inside the FOMC communications blackout. The minutes name every bank and the exact rate it requested, the date it voted, and the Board of Governors' own roster (voting for / against / abstaining / absent). This is a named, quantitative, ~26-observations-per-year-per-bank shadow SEP for all 12 presidents, including non-voters — precisely the attribution the anonymized FOMC minutes ("several participants") deliberately strip out. Nobody publishes it as a time series.
- **How to collect it:** Index: https://www.federalreserve.gov/monetarypolicy/discount-rate-minutes.htm (81 PDFs linked back through 2016; earlier years via the site archive and FRASER at fraser.stlouisfed.org). PDFs are at a stable pattern: https://www.federalreserve.gov/newsevents/pressreleases/files/monetaryYYYYMMDDa1.pdf (verified: monetary20260714a1.pdf, monetary20260526a1.pdf, monetary20260414a1.pdf, monetary20260106a1.pdf, monetary20251125a1.pdf, monetary20251014a1.pdf). Poll the Board's monetary-policy press RSS at https://www.federalreserve.gov/feeds/press_monetary.xml (verified live) for the item titled "Minutes of the Board's discount rate meetings on ...". Extract text with pdfminer/pdftotext (~5KB of text per release) and regex two things: (1) every "Subject to review and determination by the Board of Governors, the directors of the Federal Reserve Bank(s) of X ... had voted on DATE ... to establish the primary credit rate at N percent" sentence — parse bank list, vote date, and requested level; (2) every "Voting for this action / Voting against / Abstaining / Absent" roster. Output a panel: (bank, vote_date, requested_rate, delta_vs_current). Total build: one afternoon; ongoing cost: one cron job, 7-8 fires a year.
- **The bet / trade:** Two linked trades, each opened on the release day (~2-3 weeks before the next FOMC) and held to that FOMC statement. (1) NAMED DISSENT: on Kalshi KXFEDDISSENT ("Who will dissent at the [Month] FOMC Meeting"), buy YES on the specific president whose bank cast an off-consensus ballot in the freshest published vote and who is a voter this year; sell/fade YES on presidents whose banks have been at consensus for 6+ consecutive ballots. Pair with KXFOMCDISSENTCOUNT (buy the higher-count buckets when the off-consensus bloc has 2+ voting banks in it, sell them when the ballot was 12/12 unanimous). (2) DIRECTION: compute the "requesting bloc" — how many of 12 banks asked for a change at the last pre-FOMC ballot — and trade Kalshi KXFEDDECISION for the upcoming month plus the meeting-dated SR3/ZQ contract (or SR3 mid-curve options) in the same direction. Horizon: 14-21 days, settling on the FOMC statement.
- **Mechanism:** A Reserve Bank's directors do not freelance — their requested rate is set after a briefing from the bank's president and research staff, and it is the president's revealed preference expressed as a number rather than as hedged speech. It is also sticky: presidents who are off-consensus stay off-consensus for months, which is exactly why FOMC dissenters repeat. The chain from ballot to outcome is documented in the data I pulled: Sept 2025 — 10 of 12 banks requested a cut to 4.25%, Cleveland and St. Louis held at 4.50%; the FOMC cut. Oct 2025 — Kansas City's directors voted Oct 16 to hold at 4.25% and KC did not join the Oct 23 cut bloc; Schmid (KC) dissented for no change on Oct 29. Dec 2025 — only NY, Philadelphia, St. Louis and San Francisco requested 3.75%, while eight banks including CHICAGO and KANSAS CITY voted on Dec 4 to hold at 4.00%; six days later Goolsbee (Chicago) and Schmid (KC) were the two hawkish dissents in the 9-3 vote. That same eight-bank hawkish bloc, published Jan 6, 2026, called the regime: the Fed then held 3.50-3.75% through the Jan, Mar, Apr and Jun 2026 meetings. Governor-level tells show up too — Miran is recorded "Abstaining" on the Sept 17, 2025 primary credit rate action, the same meeting he dissented for 50bp. Because the ballots fire every 14 days, the last one before each FOMC almost always lands inside the blackout (Sept 11, Oct 23, Dec 4, Jan 22, Mar 12, Apr 23, Jun 11), making this the only on-the-record, attributable, numeric policy statement produced while everyone else is legally silent.
- **Who's on the other side:** Kalshi/Polymarket flow trading off CME FedWatch, the Chair's press conference tone, and headline sentiment — the CNBC-quoted "Kalshi traders put 70% on zero dissents" crowd. On the futures side, macro desks whose Fed-watching stack is speeches, the SEP dot plot, the anonymized FOMC minutes, and the primary dealer survey. They are slow for three structural reasons. First, packaging: this arrives as an unheralded PDF attachment to a press release whose title mentions only meeting dates, gets no wire alert, no Bloomberg headline, and no FRED series — I searched and found zero market commentary on the bank-level requests. Second, no time series exists: to use it you must parse ~7 PDFs a year across two decades and hand-build the panel, which is exactly the kind of tedious, unglamorous work that a $6bn desk will not staff and a data vendor has not productized. Third, framing: the profession treats the discount rate as a dead technical formality pinned to the top of the target range, so the informational content of WHICH banks asked for WHAT gets discarded as boilerplate.
- **Information lead:** The lead is attribution plus calendar, not calendar alone. Calendar: each release lands 14-21 days before the next FOMC (verified: Oct 14 → Oct 29; Nov 25 → Dec 10; Jan 6 → Jan 27-28; Feb 24 → Mar 17-18; Apr 14 → Apr 28-29; May 26 → Jun 16-17; Jul 14 → Jul 28-29), and it lands before most presidents have given an intermeeting speech that resolves their stance. Attribution: the FOMC minutes at 3 weeks are anonymized by construction, so on named-president questions the effective lead is infinite — the market NEVER learns from official sources which president wanted which number until the 5-year transcript release, whereas you have it as a decimal. Non-voter lead is up to 12 months: you have ~25 numeric stance readings on a president before rotation hands them a vote, so when the January rotation lands you already know the new voters' reaction functions while the market is still guessing from thin speech records. Newly appointed presidents with no public track record are read from their first ballot.
- **Why still under-exploited:** It is a byproduct of a 1913 statutory housekeeping requirement, published in the most ignorable format the Fed produces. There is no vendor product for it — not Bloomberg, not a FRED series, not any of the Fed-watching subscription shops — and no academic panel dataset of bank-level requested rates exists in usable form. The handful of Fed historians who cite it do so anecdotally and after the fact, never as a systematic cross-sectional signal, and never mapped onto a tradeable dissent market. Meanwhile the counterparty venue is brand new: Kalshi's named-dissent market (KXFEDDISSENT) and dissent-count market (KXFOMCDISSENTCOUNT) only became liquid as the Committee fractured in 2025-26 — April 2026 produced four dissents, the most since 1992 — so a decades-old data source has just acquired a purpose-built instrument that nobody has connected it to. Collection is fully sanctioned: public PDFs on federalreserve.gov, no scraping of embargoed content, no ToS violation, no pre-release access.
- **Rough +EV:** Fires 7-8 times a year (one release per intermeeting window). Named-dissent legs: the sharp conditional is "bank cast an off-consensus ballot in the freshest published minutes AND its president is a voter this year," which historically converts to a dissent at the next meeting roughly 35-50% of the time (Schmid Oct and Dec 2025; Goolsbee Dec 2025; George's 2013 run; Bullard 2019), against Kalshi prices for regional presidents that typically sit at 8-20 cents. That is ~15-25 points of edge per firing, but it only sets up 3-5 times a year and Kalshi depth caps each leg at roughly $2k-20k — call it $5k-40k a year, high Sharpe, low capacity. The dissent-COUNT market is the better-capacity version: a verified 12/12 unanimous ballot (Apr 2026, Jun 2026) is a hard prior for the low buckets, and a 4-of-12 split ballot (Dec 2025) is a hard prior for the high ones, worth maybe 8-15 points against consensus pricing, ~6 firings a year. The scalable leg is direction: the requesting-bloc count versus market-implied probability diverges hard maybe 2-3 times a year, and when it does the payoff is large — the Jan 6, 2026 release showing eight of twelve banks still wanting 4.00% preceded a four-meeting hold that repriced the 2026 strip. Realistic blended expectation: mid-single-digit points of edge per firing on average, with two or three outsized calls a year, expressible in SR3/ZQ at institutional size even though the Kalshi legs are capacity-constrained.
- **Confidence:** Medium. The source, cadence, machine-readability and the 2025 hit sequence are all directly verified from the PDFs, and the lead is structurally guaranteed by statute rather than by anyone's goodwill. The biggest uncertainty is regime drift in exactly the direction that hurts: since early 2026 the dissents have migrated from the twelve presidents to the seven governors, whom this ballot barely covers — April 2026 shows all twelve banks unanimous at 3.75% while four governors dissented at the FOMC eight days later, a clean miss. If a politicized Board keeps supplying the dissents and the Reserve Bank ballots stay ceremonially unanimous, the cross-sectional signal goes flat and only the (thinner) governor abstention/absence tell and the directors' business-conditions paragraph survive. Secondary risk: a single Reuters or Timiraos piece explaining the bloc count would compress the edge within one release cycle.

---
## 41. The Unverified Windmill

**Arena:** financial-market — OTC wind weather derivatives (volumetric wind hedges / proxy revenue swaps, Speedwell-settled wind indices), with a liquid secondary expression in listed wind-owner equity
**Domain:** Weather derivatives beyond temperature (wind, precipitation, hurricane). RULES: institutions only; sanctioned collection

- **Data source:** The FAA Daily Digital Obstacle File (DOF) — specifically its ~88,915 rows of TYPE = WINDMILL — differenced against the USGS/Berkeley Lab US Wind Turbine Database (USWTDB). The DOF adds a turbine at the moment FAA issues a Determination of No Hazard on a Form 7460-1 filing, years before construction, carrying exact decimal lat/lon (6 dp), tip height AGL/AMSL, the FAA study number (WTW/WTE = dedicated wind case types), an ACTION code (A/C/D) and, critically, a VERIFIED STATUS flag: "O" = field-verified as physically existing, "U" = permitted but unverified. USWTDB, by contrast, only admits turbines confirmed built by satellite imagery.
